# Workflow notebook – Add traffic-light status dashboard widget
_Backlog ID: 5d3870ad-52e1-404b-a11b-ed50438699a5_

## Enrich — 2026-04-28 19:16 UTC
- **Status:** SUCCEEDED
- **Board:** EXECUTION
- **Workflow run:** N/A

### Summary
```json
{
  "title": "Add traffic-light status dashboard widget",
  "description": "## Goal\nAdd a new dashboard widget that evaluates a configured numeric value or monitored numeric metric against thresholds and displays a traffic-light status indicator with explicit green/yellow/red, no-data, and invalid-configuration states.\n\n## Scope\nThis ticket covers a single dashboard widget type end-to-end: widget registration, persisted configuration, server-side validation, value resolution, status evaluation, refresh/update behavior, and frontend rendering. The widget must fit into the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and data retrieval path. It must not introduce a generalized alerting engine or a new telemetry/monitoring pipeline.\n\nThe widget should support:\n- A widget picker entry and persisted widget type/manifest\n- A numeric literal input or existing monitored numeric value source\n- Configurable thresholds, labels, and inclusive/exclusive comparison semantics\n- Visual rendering of green/yellow/red states plus distinct no-data and invalid-configuration states\n- Refresh behavior when the underlying value changes or the dashboard refreshes\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new dashboard widget type/manifest so the dashboard can enumerate, instantiate, and persist the widget.\n- Extend widget configuration storage/schema to hold the selected input mode, numeric literal or source reference, threshold definitions, label text, and comparison semantics.\n- Add server-side validation for required fields, supported comparison operators, threshold ordering, overlapping or malformed ranges, and source compatibility with a numeric widget input.\n- Implement backend value resolution and status evaluation in the existing widget data path so the response includes computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- Surface no-data and invalid-configuration outcomes through the normal widget response/state model.\n- Ensure the widget is wired into the existing dashboard refresh/update lifecycle so it re-evaluates when the value changes or the dashboard refreshes.\n- Register the widget in any server-side factory/registry/seed/migration path required for fresh installs and upgrades.\n- Update frontend widget picker, editor, and renderer logic so the widget appears as a supported dashboard option and renders consistent status states.\n- Update documentation listings or module enablement references if the dashboard widget registry is discoverable through those paths.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- The Traffic Light widget appears in the add-widget picker on supported installations.\n- Users can configure either a numeric value or a monitored numeric source, define thresholds and labels, save the widget, and reload it with settings preserved.\n- The widget renders green, yellow, or red according to the configured thresholds and current value.\n- The widget displays a distinct no-data state when no valid numeric value is available.\n- The widget displays a distinct invalid-configuration state when validation fails or the persisted config is incomplete/corrupt.\n- Invalid threshold configurations are rejected with a clear error message before the widget is saved.\n- The widget updates on dashboard refresh or underlying value change using the existing widget lifecycle.\n- Fresh installs and upgraded environments both expose the widget consistently.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Add the new widget type/manifest to the dashboard widget registry/factory.\n- [ ] Extend persisted widget configuration to store input mode, source reference or numeric literal, threshold bands, labels, and comparison semantics.\n- [ ] Add server-side validation for required fields, numeric parsing, threshold ordering, overlap detection, and compatible source types.\n- [ ] Implement backend value resolution from either the configured numeric literal or the selected monitored numeric source.\n- [ ] Compute widget status and metadata for green/yellow/red, no-data, and invalid-configuration outcomes.\n- [ ] Ensure widget serialization/deserialization round-trips all new fields.\n- [ ] Add registry/seed/migration updates if required for fresh installs or upgrades.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add the widget to the add-widget picker using the existing registry data source.\n- [ ] Implement the widget edit form for input mode selection, value/source selection, threshold entries, label text, and boundary behavior.\n- [ ] Reuse existing threshold field components where possible to keep the UX consistent.\n- [ ] Render the widget body with clear visual states for green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration.\n- [ ] Show the current numeric value and the label associated with the active threshold band.\n- [ ] Map backend status metadata to existing dashboard colors, icons, and styles.\n- [ ] Handle refresh events by updating the widget state without a full page reload.\n- [ ] Surface server-side validation errors inline in the widget configuration form.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Unit test backend threshold evaluation for boundary conditions, inclusive/exclusive rules, label selection, and state mapping.\n- [ ] Unit test backend configuration validation for missing fields, malformed thresholds, overlapping ranges, invalid numeric input, and incompatible source types.\n- [ ] Add backend persistence/round-trip tests to confirm saved widget settings reload exactly as configured.\n- [ ] Add API/controller tests for create, update, and read flows for the new widget type.\n- [ ] Add frontend component tests for picker visibility, edit form behavior, render states, and refresh updates.\n- [ ] Add integration tests to verify the widget can be configured, saved, reopened, and refreshed within the dashboard lifecycle.\n- [ ] Add regression coverage for upgrade or fresh-seed paths if registry, manifest, or module enablement data is required.\n- [ ] Manual verification: create the widget from the picker, save it, reload the dashboard, and confirm the saved configuration is preserved.\n- [ ] Manual verification: test each threshold boundary to confirm the color/state changes at the expected values.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify the no-data state by pointing the widget at a source with no current numeric value or by temporarily removing the source.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify invalid-configuration handling by saving overlapping or incomplete thresholds and confirming the rejection message.\n- [ ] Manual verification: trigger a dashboard refresh or underlying value update and confirm the widget re-renders without a full page reload.\n- [ ] Manual verification: check both a fresh-seeded environment and an upgraded environment if registry or seed data is involved.\n\n## Verification\n- Confirm the widget appears in the add-widget picker.\n- Save a configuration using both direct numeric input and a monitored numeric source, then reload and verify persistence.\n- Validate each threshold boundary to confirm the rendered status changes at the expected values.\n- Verify the no-data state by using a source with no current numeric value.\n- Verify the invalid-configuration state by submitting overlapping, incomplete, or malformed thresholds.\n- Refresh the dashboard or update the underlying value and confirm the widget updates without a full page reload.\n- Verify fresh-install and upgraded-environment behavior if registry, manifest, or seed data changes are required.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Update dashboard widget listings or module documentation so the new widget is documented as a supported option.\n- Update any architecture or dashboard widget docs that describe widget registration, refresh flow, or supported widget types.\n- Document the configuration semantics for threshold ordering, boundary inclusion, and no-data behavior.\n- Note any required seed, migration, or upgrade steps if the widget is introduced through registry data.\n- Add the provided PlantUML diagram to the relevant design or implementation docs if diagrams are maintained for dashboard widget flows.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\nleft to right direction\nskinparam componentStyle rectangle\nskinparam shadowing false\n\nactor \"Dashboard user\" as User\ncomponent \"Widget edit form\\n(frontend)\" as FEEdit\ncomponent \"Widget registry / manifest\" as Registry\ncomponent \"Widget config persistence\" as Persist\ncomponent \"Dashboard widget backend\" as BE\ncomponent \"Value source\\n(existing monitored value\\nor numeric literal)\" as Source\ncomponent \"Threshold evaluation\" as Eval\ncomponent \"Widget renderer\\n(frontend)\" as FERender\n\nUser --> FEEdit : configure input mode\\nsource / numeric value\\nthresholds / labels\nFEEdit --> Registry : load widget type\nFEEdit --> Persist : save config\nPersist --> BE : round-trip persisted config\nBE --> Source : resolve latest numeric value\nBE --> Eval : compare value vs thresholds\nEval --> BE : status + metadata\\n(green/yellow/red\\nno-data/invalid-config)\nBE --> FERender : widget response/state\nFERender --> User : render status color\\nvalue + label\\ninline validation/errors\n\nnote right of Eval\nExplicit outcomes:\n- green / yellow / red\n- no-data\n- invalid-configuration\nend note\n\n@enduml\n```",

/ numeric value\\nthresholds / labels\nFEEdit --> Registry : load widget type\nFEEdit --> Persist : save config\nPersist --> BE : round-trip persisted config\nBE --> Source : resolve latest numeric               "codex_prompt": "## Goal\nAdd a new dashboard widget that evaluates a configured numeric value or monitored numeric metric against thresholds and displays a traffic-light status indicator with explicit green/yellow/red, no-data, and invalid-configuration states.\n\n## Scope\nThis ticket covers a single dashboard widget type end-to-end: widget registration, persisted configuration, server-side validation, value resolution, status evaluation, refresh/update behavior, and frontend rendering. The widget must fit into the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and data retrieval path. It must not introduce a generalized alerting engine or a new telemetry/monitoring pipeline.\n\nThe widget should support:\n- A widget picker entry and persisted widget type/manifest\n- A numeric literal input or existing monitored numeric value source\n- Configurable thresholds, labels, and inclusive/exclusive comparison semantics\n- Visual rendering of green/yellow/red states plus distinct no-data and invalid-configuration states\n- Refresh behavior when the underlying value changes or the dashboard refreshes\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new dashboard widget type/manifest so the dashboard can enumerate, instantiate, and persist the widget.\n- Extend widget configuration storage/schema to hold the selected input mode, numeric literal or source reference, threshold definitions, label text, and comparison semantics.\n- Add server-side validation for required fields, supported comparison operators, threshold ordering, overlapping or malformed ranges, and source compatibility with a numeric widget input.\n- Implement backend value resolution and status evaluation in the existing widget data path so the response includes computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- Surface no-data and invalid-configuration outcomes through the normal widget response/state model.\n- Ensure the widget is wired into the existing dashboard refresh/update lifecycle so it re-evaluates when the value changes or the dashboard refreshes.\n- Register the widget in any server-side factory/registry/seed/migration path required for fresh installs and upgrades.\n- Update frontend widget picker, editor, and renderer logic so the widget appears as a supported dashboard option and renders consistent status states.\n- Update documentation listings or module enablement references if the dashboard widget registry is discoverable through those paths.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- The Traffic Light widget appears in the add-widget picker on supported installations.\n- Users can configure either a numeric value or a monitored numeric source, define thresholds and labels, save the widget, and reload it with settings preserved.\n- The widget renders green, yellow, or red according to the configured thresholds and current value.\n- The widget displays a distinct no-data state when no valid numeric value is available.\n- The widget displays a distinct invalid-configuration state when validation fails or the persisted config is incomplete/corrupt.\n- Invalid threshold configurations are rejected with a clear error message before the widget is saved.\n- The widget updates on dashboard refresh or underlying value change using the existing widget lifecycle.\n- Fresh installs and upgraded environments both expose the widget consistently.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Add the new widget type/manifest to the dashboard widget registry/factory.\n- [ ] Extend persisted widget configuration to store input mode, source reference or numeric literal, threshold bands, labels, and comparison semantics.\n- [ ] Add server-side validation for required fields, numeric parsing, threshold ordering, overlap detection, and compatible source types.\n- [ ] Implement backend value resolution from either the configured numeric literal or the selected monitored numeric source.\n- [ ] Compute widget status and metadata for green/yellow/red, no-data, and invalid-configuration outcomes.\n- [ ] Ensure widget serialization/deserialization round-trips all new fields.\n- [ ] Add registry/seed/migration updates if required for fresh installs or upgrades.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add the widget to the add-widget picker using the existing registry data source.\n- [ ] Implement the widget edit form for input mode selection, value/source selection, threshold entries, label text, and boundary behavior.\n- [ ] Reuse existing threshold field components where possible to keep the UX consistent.\n- [ ] Render the widget body with clear visual states for green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration.\n- [ ] Show the current numeric value and the label associated with the active threshold band.\n- [ ] Map backend status metadata to existing dashboard colors, icons, and styles.\n- [ ] Handle refresh events by updating the widget state without a full page reload.\n- [ ] Surface server-side validation errors inline in the widget configuration form.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Unit test backend threshold evaluation for boundary conditions, inclusive/exclusive rules, label selection, and state mapping.\n- [ ] Unit test backend configuration validation for missing fields, malformed thresholds, overlapping ranges, invalid numeric input, and incompatible source types.\n- [ ] Add backend persistence/round-trip tests to confirm saved widget settings reload exactly as configured.\n- [ ] Add API/controller tests for create, update, and read flows for the new widget type.\n- [ ] Add frontend component tests for picker visibility, edit form behavior, render states, and refresh updates.\n- [ ] Add integration tests to verify the widget can be configured, saved, reopened, and refreshed within the dashboard lifecycle.\n- [ ] Add regression coverage for upgrade or fresh-seed paths if registry, manifest, or module enablement data is required.\n- [ ] Manual verification: create the widget from the picker, save it, reload the dashboard, and confirm the saved configuration is preserved.\n- [ ] Manual verification: test each threshold boundary to confirm the color/state changes at the expected values.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify the no-data state by pointing the widget at a source with no current numeric value or by temporarily removing the source.\n- [ ] Manual verification: verify invalid-configuration handling by saving overlapping or incomplete thresholds and confirming the rejection message.\n- [ ] Manual verification: trigger a dashboard refresh or underlying value update and confirm the widget re-renders without a full page reload.\n- [ ] Manual verification: check both a fresh-seeded environment and an upgraded environment if registry or seed data is involved.\n\n## reload.\n- Verify fresh-install and upgraded-environment behavior if registry, manifest, or seed data changes are required.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Update dashboard widget listings or module label text, and comparison semantics.\n- Add server-side validation for required fields, supported comparison operators, threshold ordering, overlapping or malformed ranges, and source compatibility with a numeric widget input.\n- Implement backend value resolution and status evaluation in the existing widget data path so the response includes computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- Surface no-data and invalid-configuration outcomes through the normal widget response/state model.\n- Ensure the widget is wired into the existing dashboard refresh/update lifecycle so it re-evaluates when the value changes or the dashboard refreshes.\n- Register the widget in any server-side factory/registry/seed/migration path required for fresh installs and upgrades.\n- Update frontend widget picker, editor, and renderer logic so the widget appears as a supported dashboard option and renders consistent status states.\n- Update documentation listings or module enablement references if the dashboard widget registry is discoverable through those paths.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- The Traffic Light widget appears in the add-widget picker on supported installations.\n- Users can configure either a numeric value or a monitored numeric source, define thresholds and labels, save the widget, and reload it with settings preserved.\n- The widget renders green, yellow, or red according to the configured thresholds and current value.\n- The widget displays a distinct no-data state when no valid numeric value is available.\n- The widget displays a distinct invalid-configuration state when validation fails or the persisted config is incomplete/corrupt.\n- Invalid threshold configurations are rejected with a clear error message before the widget is saved.\n- The widget updates on dashboard refresh or underlying value change using the existing widget lifecycle.\n- Fresh installs and upgraded environments both expose the widget consistently.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n### Backend\n- [ ] Add the new widget type/manifest to the dashboard widget registry/factory.\n- [ ] Extend persisted widget configuration to store input mode, source reference or numeric literal, Implement backend value resolution from either the configured numeric literal or the selected monitored numeric source.\n- [ ] Compute widget status and metadata for green/yellow/red, no-data, and invalid-configuration outcomes.\n- [ ] Ensure widget serialization/deserialization round-trips all new fields.\n- [ ] Add registry/seed/migration updates if required for fresh installs or upgrades.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add the widget to the add-widget picker using the existing registry data source.\n- [ ] Implement the widget edit form for input mode selection, value/source selection, threshold entries, label text, and boundary behavior.\n- [ ] Reuse existing threshold field components where possible to keep the UX consistent.\n- [ ] Render the widget body with clear visual states for green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration.\n- [ ] Show the current numeric value and the label associated with the active threshold band.\n- [ ] Map backend status metadata to existing dashboard colors, icons, and styles.\n- [ ] Handle refresh events by updating the widget state without a full page reload.\n- [ ] Surface server-side validation errors inline in the widget configuration form.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Unit test backend threshold evaluation for boundary conditions, inclusive/exclusive rules, label selection, and state mapping.\n- [ ] Unit test backend configuration validation for missing fields, malformed thresholds, overlapping ranges, invalid numeric input, and incompatible source types.\n- [ ] Add backend persistence/round-trip tests to confirm saved widget settings reload exactly as configured.\n- [ ] Add API/controller tests for create, update, and read flows for the new widget type.\n- [ ] Add frontend component tests for picker visibility, edit form behavior, render states, and refresh updates.\n- [ ] Add integration tests to verify the widget can be configured, saved, reopened, and refreshed within the dashboard lifecycle.\n- [ ] Add regression coverage for upgrade or fresh-seed paths if registry, manifest, or module enablement data is required.\n- [ ] Manual verification: create the widget from the picker, {
  "title": "Add threshold-based traffic-light dashboard widget",
  "description": "## Goal\nAdd a new dashboard widget that evaluates a single configured numeric input against thresholds and displays a traffic-light status indicator with explicit green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration states.\n\n## Scope\nThis ticket covers one dashboard widget type end-to-end: registration, persisted configuration, server-side validation, value resolution, status evaluation, refresh/update behavior, and frontend rendering. The widget must fit into the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and data retrieval path. It must not introduce a generalized alerting engine or a new telemetry/monitoring pipeline.\n\nThe widget should support:\n- A widget picker entry and persisted widget type/manifest\n- A numeric literal input or existing monitored numeric value source\n- Configurable thresholds, labels, and inclusive/exclusive comparison semantics\n- Visual rendering of green/yellow/red states plus distinct no-data and invalid-configuration states\n- Refresh behavior when the underlying value changes or the dashboard refreshes\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new dashboard widget type/manifest so the dashboard registry can enumerate, instantiate, and persist the widget.\n- Extend widget configuration storage/schema to hold the selected input mode, numeric literal or source reference, threshold definitions, label text, and comparison semantics.\n- Add server-side validation for required fields, numeric constraints, threshold ordering, overlapping or malformed ranges, and source compatibility with a numeric widget input.\n- Implement backend value resolution and status evaluation in the existing widget data path so the response includes computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- Surface no-data and invalid-configuration outcomes through the normal widget response/state model.\n- Ensure the widget is wired into the existing dashboard refresh/update lifecycle so it re-evaluates when the value changes or the dashboard refreshes.\n- Register the widget in any server-side factory/registry/seed/migration path required for fresh installs and upgrades.\n- Update frontend widget picker, editor, and renderer logic so the widget appears as a supported dashboard option and renders consistent status states.\n- Update documentation listings or module enablement references if the dashboard widget registry is discoverable through those paths.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- The new traffic-light widget appears in the dashboard add-widget/type picker.\n- A user can configure the widget using either a numeric literal or a supported numeric source.\n- The widget persists and reloads its configuration correctly.\n- The backend validates configuration and returns explicit invalid-configuration for malformed setups.\n- The widget evaluates the resolved numeric value against thresholds and displays green, yellow, or red correctly.\n- The widget displays explicit no-data when no numeric value is available.\n- The widget refreshes correctly on dashboard refresh and when the underlying value changes according to existing lifecycle behavior.\n- Fresh installs and upgraded databases both include the widget in the registry/manifest path.\n- The frontend renders all required states consistently and does not require the user to infer status from color alone.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n\n### Backend\n- [ ] Identify the existing dashboard widget type registration/factory and add the new widget type there.\n- [ ] Add persistence fields or JSON schema support for the widget configuration, including selected input mode, literal value or source reference, thresholds, labels, and comparison mode.\n- [ ] Implement backend resolution of the input value from either a literal numeric config or a linked monitored numeric source.\n- [ ] Implement status evaluation logic that maps the resolved numeric value to green/yellow/red based on configured thresholds and emits no-data when no value is available.\n- [ ] Return invalid-configuration when required fields are missing, incompatible, or semantically invalid.\n- [ ] Validate that referenced source widgets or monitored values are compatible numeric inputs and fail cleanly otherwise.\n- [ ] Ensure backend responses include enough metadata for frontend rendering, such as current status, resolved value, and any warning or state reason.\n- [ ] Wire refresh behavior into the widget data loading path so cached or stale values are not used beyond the normal dashboard refresh cadence.\n- [ ] Add any migration or seed changes needed for widget manifest registration in existing installs and fresh databases.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add the widget to the dashboard add-widget/type picker with the correct label and icon/preview if applicable.\n- [ ] Build or extend the widget editor form to support input mode selection, numeric literal entry or source selection, threshold configuration, labels, and inclusive/exclusive toggle settings.\n- [ ] Show validation feedback in the editor for malformed thresholds, missing required values, or incompatible source selection.\n- [ ] Render the widget status states consistently for green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration.\n- [ ] Ensure the widget renderer consumes the backend-provided status and metadata rather than duplicating business logic in the client.\n- [ ] Make the widget update when the dashboard refreshes or when the underlying value source changes per existing widget lifecycle behavior.\n- [ ] Ensure any empty or invalid states use the same visual language as other dashboard widgets, including cover messages or placeholder states if that is the established pattern.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for threshold evaluation across green/yellow/red boundaries, including inclusive and exclusive comparisons.\n- [ ] Add backend tests for no-data handling when the numeric source is missing or unresolved.\n- [ ] Add backend tests for invalid-configuration cases such as missing fields, malformed thresholds, reversed bounds, or unsupported source types.\n- [ ] Add persistence or migration tests to confirm the widget configuration is saved and loaded correctly.\n- [ ] Add API/integration coverage that verifies the backend response includes the resolved value, status, and state reason metadata.\n- [ ] Add UI/editor tests for widget picker visibility and form behavior when switching between literal and source input modes.\n- [ ] Add integration or end-to-end coverage that creates the widget, configures it, refreshes the dashboard, and verifies rendered state transitions.\n- [ ] Add regression coverage to ensure the widget appears in the dashboard add-widget modal for both fresh installs and upgraded databases.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Create the widget from the dashboard UI and confirm it is present in the type picker.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Configure it once with a literal number and once with a monitored numeric source, then verify the rendered status matches the thresholds.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Force a no-data condition and verify the widget shows the no-data state instead of failing generically.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Enter an invalid configuration and verify the widget displays invalid-configuration with server-side validation feedback.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Refresh the dashboard and verify the widget re-evaluates.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Verify the widget is available after a clean install seed path and after an upgrade path.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Check logs or backend response payloads to confirm value resolution and status evaluation happen server-side.\n\n## Verification\n- Automated tests confirm threshold evaluation, state mapping, persistence, and validation.\n- API/integration checks confirm the backend returns computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- UI tests confirm the widget can be discovered, configured, and edited in both literal and source modes.\n- Manual verification confirms the end-to-end dashboard behavior, including refresh behavior, no-data handling, invalid-configuration handling, and visible rendering states.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Update widget/module listing documentation if the dashboard registry is documented or generated from manifests.\n- Update any dashboard widget configuration docs to describe the new traffic-light widget inputs, thresholds, and state meanings.\n- Update architecture or data-flow notes if there is a documented dashboard widget lifecycle or widget state model.\n- If the project maintains upgrade notes or seed/migration documentation, note the new widget registration path there.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\nleft to right direction\nskinparam shadowing false\nskinparam componentStyle rectangle\nskinparam ArrowColor #555\n\nactor \"User\" as U\ncomponent \"Add-widget picker\" as Picker\ncomponent \"Widget editor\" as Editor\ndatabase \"Dashboard config store\" as Store\ncomponent \"Widget registry / factory\" as Reg\ncomponent \"Backend value resolver\" as Resolve\ncomponent \"Threshold evaluator\" as Eval\ncomponent \"Widget API response\" as Resp\ncomponent \"Frontend renderer\" as Render\ncomponent \"Refresh lifecycle\" as Refresh\n\nU --> Picker : choose Traffic Light\nPicker --> Editor : open config form\nEditor --> Store : persist input mode\\nliteral/source ref\\nthresholds, labels, inclusive/exclusive\nStore --> Reg : load widget config\nReg --> Resolve : instantiate widget type\nResolve --> Eval : resolve numeric value\n\nResolve --> Resp : no-data\nResolve --> Resp : invalid-configuration\nEval --> Resp : green / yellow / red\nEval --> Resp : resolved value + status + reason\n\nResp --> Render : render state\nRender --> Refresh : subscribe to dashboard refresh\\n/ source change updates\nRefresh --> Resolve : re-evaluate on refresh\n\nnote right of Resolve\nBackend enforces:\n- required fields\n- numeric compatibility\n- threshold ordering / semantics\n- clean failure states\nend note\n\nnote bottom of Render\nFrontend does not duplicate status logic;\nit displays backend-provided state,\nincluding no-data and invalid-configuration.\nend note\n@enduml\n```",
  "codex_prompt": "## Goal\nAdd a new dashboard widget that evaluates a single configured numeric input against thresholds and displays a traffic-light status indicator with explicit green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration states.\n\n## Scope\nThis ticket covers one dashboard widget type end-to-end: registration, persisted configuration, server-side validation, value resolution, status evaluation, refresh/update behavior, and frontend rendering. The widget must fit into the existing dashboard widget lifecycle and data retrieval path. It must not introduce a generalized alerting engine or a new telemetry/monitoring pipeline.\n\nThe widget should support:\n- A widget picker entry and persisted widget type/manifest\n- A numeric literal input or existing monitored numeric value source\n- Configurable thresholds, labels, and inclusive/exclusive comparison semantics\n- Visual rendering of green/yellow/red states plus distinct no-data and invalid-configuration states\n- Refresh behavior when the underlying value changes or the dashboard refreshes\n\n## System Changes\n- Add a new dashboard widget type/manifest so the dashboard registry can enumerate, instantiate, and persist the widget.\n- Extend widget configuration storage/schema to hold the selected input mode, numeric literal or source reference, threshold definitions, label text, and comparison semantics.\n- Add server-side validation for required fields, numeric constraints, threshold ordering, overlapping or malformed ranges, and source compatibility with a numeric widget input.\n- Implement backend value resolution and status evaluation in the existing widget data path so the response includes computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- Surface no-data and invalid-configuration outcomes through the normal widget response/state model.\n- Ensure the widget is wired into the existing dashboard refresh/update lifecycle so it re-evaluates when the value changes or the dashboard refreshes.\n- Register the widget in any server-side factory/registry/seed/migration path required for fresh installs and upgrades.\n- Update frontend widget picker, editor, and renderer logic so the widget appears as a supported dashboard option and renders consistent status states.\n- Update documentation listings or module enablement references if the dashboard widget registry is discoverable through those paths.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- The new traffic-light widget appears in the dashboard add-widget/type picker.\n- A user can configure the widget using either a numeric literal or a supported numeric source.\n- The widget persists and reloads its configuration correctly.\n- The backend validates configuration and returns explicit invalid-configuration for malformed setups.\n- The widget evaluates the resolved numeric value against thresholds and displays green, yellow, or red correctly.\n- The widget displays explicit no-data when no numeric value is available.\n- The widget refreshes correctly on dashboard refresh and when the underlying value changes according to existing lifecycle behavior.\n- Fresh installs and upgraded databases both include the widget in the registry/manifest path.\n- The frontend renders all required states consistently and does not require the user to infer status from color alone.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n\n### Backend\n- [ ] Identify the existing dashboard widget type registration/factory and add the new widget type there.\n- [ ] Add persistence fields or JSON schema support for the widget configuration, including selected input mode, literal value or source reference, thresholds, labels, and comparison mode.\n- [ ] Implement backend resolution of the input value from either a literal numeric config or a linked monitored numeric source.\n- [ ] Implement status evaluation logic that maps the resolved numeric value to green/yellow/red based on configured thresholds and emits no-data when no value is available.\n- [ ] Return invalid-configuration when required fields are missing, incompatible, or semantically invalid.\n- [ ] Validate that referenced source widgets or monitored values are compatible numeric inputs and fail cleanly otherwise.\n- [ ] Ensure backend responses include enough metadata for frontend rendering, such as current status, resolved value, and any warning or state reason.\n- [ ] Wire refresh behavior into the widget data loading path so cached or stale values are not used beyond the normal dashboard refresh cadence.\n- [ ] Add any migration or seed changes needed for widget manifest registration in existing installs and fresh databases.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add the widget to the dashboard add-widget/type picker with the correct label and icon/preview if applicable.\n- [ ] Build or extend the widget editor form to support input mode selection, numeric literal entry or source selection, threshold configuration, labels, and inclusive/exclusive toggle settings.\n- [ ] Show validation feedback in the editor for malformed thresholds, missing required values, or incompatible source selection.\n- [ ] Render the widget status states consistently for green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration.\n- [ ] Ensure the widget renderer consumes the backend-provided status and metadata rather than duplicating business logic in the client.\n- [ ] Make the widget update when the dashboard refreshes or when the underlying value source changes per existing widget lifecycle behavior.\n- [ ] Ensure any empty or invalid states use the same visual language as other dashboard widgets, including cover messages or placeholder states if that is the established pattern.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Add backend unit tests for threshold evaluation across green/yellow/red boundaries, including inclusive and exclusive comparisons.\n- [ ] Add backend tests for no-data handling when the numeric source is missing or unresolved.\n- [ ] Add backend tests for invalid-configuration cases such as missing fields, malformed thresholds, reversed bounds, or unsupported source types.\n- [ ] Add persistence or migration tests to confirm the widget configuration is saved and loaded correctly.\n- [ ] Add API/integration coverage that verifies the backend response includes the resolved value, status, and state reason metadata.\n- [ ] Add UI/editor tests for widget picker visibility and form behavior when switching between literal and source input modes.\n- [ ] Add integration or end-to-end coverage that creates the widget, configures it, refreshes the dashboard, and verifies rendered state transitions.\n- [ ] Add regression coverage to ensure the widget appears in the dashboard add-widget modal for both fresh installs and upgraded databases.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Create the widget from the dashboard UI and confirm it is present in the type picker.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Configure it once with a literal number and once with a monitored numeric source, then verify the rendered status matches the thresholds.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Force a no-data condition and verify the widget shows the no-data state instead of failing generically.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Enter an invalid configuration and verify the widget displays invalid-configuration with server-side validation feedback.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Refresh the dashboard and verify the widget re-evaluates.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Verify the widget is available after a clean install seed path and after an upgrade path.\n- [ ] Manual verification: Check logs or backend response payloads to confirm value resolution and status evaluation happen server-side.\n\n## Verification\n- Automated tests confirm threshold evaluation, state mapping, persistence, and validation.\n- API/integration checks confirm the backend returns computed status plus supporting metadata.\n- UI tests confirm the widget can be discovered, configured, and edited in both literal and source modes.\n- Manual verification confirms the end-to-end dashboard behavior, including refresh behavior, no-data handling, invalid-configuration handling, and visible rendering states.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Update widget/module listing documentation if the dashboard registry is documented or generated from manifests.\n- Update any dashboard widget configuration docs to describe the new traffic-light widget inputs, thresholds, and state meanings.\n- Update architecture or data-flow notes if there is a documented dashboard widget lifecycle or widget state model.\n- If the project maintains upgrade notes or seed/migration documentation, note the new widget registration path there.\n\n## Suggested type/manifest and register it in the widget factory/registry so it appears in the add-widget picker and persists correctly.\n- Extend widget configuration schema/storage to capture input mode, numeric literal or source reference, thresholds, labels, and inclusive/exclusive comparison settings.\n- Add backend validation for required fields, numeric compatibility, threshold ordering, overlap/malformed ranges, and unsupported source types.\n- Implement value resolution and status evaluation in the existing widget data retrieval path so the API response includes computed status and supporting metadata.\n- Propagate no-data and invalid-configuration outcomes through the existing widget state model and refresh lifecycle.\n- Add fresh-install seed and upgrade migration hooks so the widget is discoverable on new and existing deployments.\n- Update frontend widget picker, editor, and renderer integration so the widget can be created, edited, refreshed, and rendered consistently.\n- Update any registry-discovery documentation or module-enablement references that drive widget availability.\n\n## Acceptance Criteria\n- Users can add the new traffic-light widget from the dashboard widget picker.\n- The widget persists and reloads with its configured literal or source-based numeric input and threshold settings.\n- Valid configurations evaluate to exactly one of green, yellow, or red based on the current value and comparison rules.\n- Missing data shows a distinct no-data state.\n- Invalid or incomplete configuration shows a distinct invalid-configuration state rather than failing silently.\n- The widget updates on dashboard refresh and when the underlying value changes.\n- Fresh installs and upgraded databases both expose the widget without manual database edits.\n- Server-side validation rejects malformed thresholds and unsupported source types.\n\n## Implementation Checklist\n\n### Backend\n- [ ] Define the widget manifest/type identifier, default configuration, and server-side serialization contract.\n- [ ] Extend persistence for the widget config fields, including migration support if the configuration is stored in a structured table or JSON blob.\n- [ ] Implement validation for threshold definitions, range semantics, labels, and source reference compatibility.\n- [ ] Resolve the configured value from either a literal numeric input or an existing monitored numeric source using the current dashboard/widget data path.\n- [ ] Evaluate the resolved value against thresholds and produce a normalized status payload with green/yellow/red/no-data/invalid-config states.\n- [ ] Emit stable metadata needed by the renderer, such as selected threshold band, evaluated value, and diagnostic message for invalid/no-data cases.\n- [ ] Wire the widget into backend registry/factory/seed/migration code paths used by the dashboard subsystem.\n- [ ] Add backend unit and API/integration tests for validation, evaluation, and response shaping.\n\n### Frontend\n- [ ] Add the widget to the add-widget type picker and ensure it uses the correct manifest/type identifier.\n- [ ] Build or extend the widget editor form to support literal input vs monitored source, threshold values, labels, and inclusive/exclusive semantics.\n- [ ] Reuse existing threshold and numeric field components where possible to avoid duplicating widget-field logic.\n- [ ] Render the widget states distinctly for green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid-configuration, including cover or empty-state messaging where appropriate.\n- [ ] Ensure the widget refreshes visually when new data arrives or the dashboard refreshes without requiring a full page reload.\n- [ ] Keep editor validation and displayed help text aligned with server-side validation rules.\n- [ ] Add frontend component/UI tests for picker visibility, editor behavior, and rendered status states.\n\n### Testing\n- [ ] Backend unit tests for threshold comparison semantics, boundary inclusivity/exclusivity, and invalid/malformed config detection.\n- [ ] Backend tests for source compatibility checks and resolution of numeric literals versus monitored numeric sources.\n- [ ] API or integration tests that verify the widget response includes computed status, no-data, and invalid-config states.\n- [ ] UI/component tests for widget configuration inputs and renderer state transitions.\n- [ ] End-to-end dashboard tests to confirm the widget can be added, saved, reopened, and refreshed correctly.\n- [ ] Regression coverage ensuring the widget appears in the add-widget modal after fresh install and after upgrade.\n- [ ] Manual verification: create a widget with a fixed numeric literal and verify each threshold band by changing the value.\n- [ ] Manual verification: bind the widget to an existing numeric source and confirm it updates on refresh.\n- [ ] Manual verification: test boundary values at exact threshold edges to confirm inclusive/exclusive behavior.\n- [ ] Manual verification: remove or break required configuration fields and verify invalid-configuration rendering and backend validation.\n- [ ] Manual verification: use a source with no current numeric value and verify no-data behavior.\n- [ ] Manual verification: confirm the widget appears in the add-widget modal after a clean seed and after running upgrade paths.\n- [ ] Manual verification: check that save/reopen preserves the exact configuration and evaluation outcome.\n\n## Verification\n- Confirm the widget can be selected from the dashboard add-widget modal and saved successfully.\n- Verify the widget reloads with the exact persisted configuration after reopening the dashboard.\n- Exercise each state band with known values: green, yellow, red, no-data, and invalid configuration.\n- Validate boundary behavior at exact threshold edges for inclusive and exclusive comparisons.\n- Confirm dashboard refresh and underlying value changes trigger updated rendering without a full page reload.\n- Confirm fresh-install and upgrade flows both expose the widget without manual database edits.\n- Confirm server-side validation rejects malformed thresholds, missing required fields, and unsupported source types.\n\n## Documentation Impact\n- Update dashboard widget listings or module registration docs if those are used to discover available widgets.\n- Document the supported input modes, threshold semantics, and state meanings for the new widget.\n- If applicable, update release notes or changelog entries describing the new dashboard widget.\n- Add a brief architecture note if the widget reuses an existing numeric-source path in a non-obvious way.\n\n## Suggested Diagram\n```puml\n@startuml\ntitle Traffic-light dashboard widget: load, evaluate, render\n\nactor User\nparticipant \"Add widget modal\" as Picker\nparticipant \"Dashboard widget registry\" as Registry\nparticipant \"Widget config store\" as Store\nparticipant \"Dashboard API / backend\" as Backend\nparticipant \"Threshold evaluator\" as Eval\nparticipant \"Widget renderer\" as Render\n\nUser -> Picker: select \"Traffic Light\"\nPicker -> Registry: resolve manifest/type id\nRegistry --> Picker: default config + field schema\nUser -> Picker: enter source/literal, thresholds, labels, semantics\nPicker -> Store: persist widget config\n\nStore -> Backend: load widget config on dashboard refresh\nBackend -> Backend: validate required fields\nBackend -> Backend: validate numeric compatibility\\nthreshold ordering / overlap / semantics\nalt invalid configuration\n  Backend -> Eval: mark invalid-config + diagnostic\n  Eval --> Backend: normalized status = invalid-config\nelse valid configuration\n  Backend -> Backend: resolve numeric value\\n(literal or monitored source)\n  alt no data / unresolved\n    Backend -> Eval: mark no-data\n    Eval --> Backend: normalized status = no-data\n  else value resolved\n    Backend -> Eval: compare value against thresholds\n    Eval --> Backend: status = green/yellow/red\\nselected band + evaluated value\n  end\nend\nBackend --> Render: status payload + metadata\nRender -> Render: show green/yellow/red/no-data/invalid state\n\nUser -> Backend: dashboard refresh / auto-refresh\nBackend --> Render: updated status payload\nRender -> Render: update widget without full reload\n\n@enduml\n```",

id\nRegistry --> Picker: default config + field schema\nUser -> Picker: enter source/literal, thresholds, labels, semantics\nPicker -> Store: persist widget config\n\nStore -> Backend: load widget config on dashboard refresh\nBackend -> Backend: validate required fields\nBackend -> Backend: validate numeric compatibility\\nthreshold ordering / overlap / semantics\nalt invalid configuration\n  Backend -> Eval: mark invalid-config + diagnostic\n  Eval --> Backend: normalized status = invalid-config\nelse valid configuration\n  Backend -> Backend: resolve numeric value\\n(literal or monitored source)\n  alt no data / unresolved\n    Backend -> Eval: mark no-data\n    Eval --> Backend: normalized status = no-data\n  else value resolved\n    Backend -> Eval: compare value against thresholds\n    Eval --> Backend: status = green/yellow/red\\nselected band + evaluated value\n  end\nend\nBackend --> Render: status payload + metadata\nRender -> Render: show green/yellow/red/no-data/invalid state\n\nUser -> Backend: dashboard refresh / auto-refresh\nBackend --> Render: updated status payload\nRender -> Render: update widget without full reload\n\n@enduml\n```",
    "rationale": "This version tightens the scope to one widget lifecycle, makes backend/frontend/test boundaries explicit, and separates automated verification from manual UI checks so implementation and QA are easier to execute. It also aligns the ticket with the provided architecture flow and includes the diagram to clarify validation, evaluation, and rendering responsibilities."
  }
}
```
